--- Model Response ---
To solve this problem, we need to identify the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to disconnect the two black nodes (A and I).

Let's analyze the connections between the black nodes A and I:

1. A is connected to:
   - P
   - Q
   - B

2. I is connected to:
   - Q
   - J
   - H
   - N

3. The connections that span between A and I through other nodes are:
   - A-P-Q-I
   - A-Q-I
   - A-B-H-N-I

To completely disconnect A and I, we need to cut all these paths. The key nodes that, when removed, will cut all paths are:

- Removing node Q will cut the paths A-P-Q-I and A-Q-I.
- Removing node B will cut the path A-B-H-N-I.

By removing nodes Q and B, all paths between A and I are cut.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2.

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [Q, B]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 1, 'cut_set': ['H']}

Here is the reasoning:

1. First, identify all paths from node R to node I. By tracing the graph:
   - Path 1: R -> J -> Q -> I
   - Path 2: R -> C -> B -> T -> F -> S -> E -> A -> N -> C -> H -> Q -> I
   - Path 3: R -> C -> G -> B -> T -> F -> S -> E -> A -> N -> C -> H -> Q -> I
   - Path 4: R -> C -> G -> B -> T -> F -> S -> E -> A -> N -> C -> J -> Q -> I

2. To disconnect R and I, we need to remove edges that appear in all paths, which are minimal and will ensure no alternative remaining paths exist.

3. Removing the edges:
   - (C, J)
   - (J, Q)
   - (C, G)

These three edge removals will ensure no path remains between the nodes R and I.

Thus, the answer is:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(C, J), (J, Q), (C, G)].

Let's trace the paths between P and J:

1. P -> A -> B -> I -> Q -> J
2. P -> B -> I -> Q -> J
3. P -> B -> E -> S -> Q -> J
4. P -> B -> E -> S -> J
5. P -> B -> C -> T -> O -> N -> H -> I -> Q -> J
6. P -> B -> C -> T -> O -> N -> H -> Q -> J

To disconnect P and J, we need to remove nodes such that all these paths are broken. We observe that removing node B can potentially cut off many of these paths. Let's check if removing just B is sufficient:

- P -> A -> B -> I -> Q -> J (removed)
- P -> B -> I -> Q -> J (removed)
- P -> B -> E -> S -> Q -> J (removed)
- P -> B -> E -> S -> J (removed)
- P -> B -> C -> T -> O -> N -> H -> I -> Q -> J (removed)
- P -> B -> C -> T -> O -> N -> H -> Q -> J (removed)

After removing B, all paths between P and J are disconnected. No other single node removal can achieve this. Therefore, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 1, and the cut set is [B].

Nodes to remove: 1, Cut Set: [B]

### Identifying Paths between E and J:

1. **Direct Paths from E to J:**
   - E -> B -> C -> N -> O -> H -> I -> P -> Q -> R -> J
   - E -> B -> C -> H -> I -> P -> Q -> R -> J
   - E -> B -> C -> H -> I -> Q -> R -> J
   - E -> B -> C -> H -> G -> T -> F -> A -> S
   - E -> B -> C -> N -> O -> H -> G -> T -> F -> A -> S
   - E -> B -> F -> T -> G -> H -> I -> P -> Q -> R -> J
   - E -> B -> F -> T -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> R -> J

### Cut Set Analysis:

By examining all possible edges involved in these paths, we observe that the edges that, if removed, will eliminate all possible paths between E and J, are:

- Remove (H, G)
- Remove (I, P)
- Remove (B, C)

This ensures that all paths between E and J are severed with the minimum number of edges.

### Conclusion:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(H, G), (I, P), (B, C)].
